1: # 🔱 Omega Engine — Sovereign Mandates
2: **Version**: 3.7.0
3: **Status**: NON-NEGOTIABLE
4: **Scope**: All Agents, All CLIs, All IDEs
5: **Updated**: 2026-07-19 (Added M24 Venv Sovereignty, M25 Streaming Resilience)
6: 
7: These mandates are the "Constitutional Law" of the Omega Engine. They override any tool-specific defaults or model-suggested patterns.
8: 
9: ## 🛡️ The Twenty-Three Laws of Sovereign Execution
10: 
11: ### 1. AnyIO Absolute
12: - **Mandate**: All asynchronous code MUST use AnyIO. 
13: - **Constraint**: Never use `asyncio` directly. 
14: - **Pattern**: Wrap blocking I/O in `anyio.to_thread.run_sync`.
15: - **Reason**: Ensures runtime portability and prevents event-loop collisions across the Provider Fabric.
16: 
17: ### 2. The Engine-Stack Firewall
18: - **Mandate**: Maintain absolute separation between the **Omega Engine Core** and **Expansion Stacks (WADs)**.
19: - **Core**: `src/omega/`, `config/omega.yaml`, `opencode.json`. (The universal runtime).
20: - **Stacks**: `config/wads/<stack_name>/`. (The specific implementation).
21: - **Constraint**: Never add stack-specific logic (e.g., a specific entity's trait) to the Core Engine.
22: - **Reason**: Prevents architectural drift and ensures the engine remains a universal runtime.
23: 
24: ### 3. The Iris Constant
25: - **Mandate**: Iris is the messenger bridge, NOT a Pillar Keeper.
26: - **Constraint**: Do not assign Iris a Pillar (P1-P10). She is the interface.
27: - **Reason**: Preserves the cosmological purity of the 10 Pillar Keepers.
28: 
29: ### 4. The Sequentiality Mandate
30: - **Mandate**: Complex architectural changes must follow the "Plan → Verify → Execute" loop.
31: - **Constraint**: No "cowboy coding." Every major edit must be preceded by a plan that is verified against the `PIVOT_LOG.md`.
32: - **Reason**: Prevents the "Restart Cycle" that plagued previous versions of the engine.
33: 
34: ### 5. Gnosis Preservation (L1 → L2 → L3)
35: - **Mandate**: No intelligence is discarded.
36: - **Constraint**: Every session must end with a distillation of findings into the entity's `soul.yaml` using the 3-tier abstraction:
37:     - **L1 (Narrative)**: What happened?
38:     - **L2 (Insight)**: What does this mean?
39:     - **L3 (Universal Principle)**: What is the timeless truth?
40: - **Reason**: Transforms stateless agent interactions into a stateful, evolving sovereign intelligence.
41: 
42: ### 6. Podman Sovereignty (keep-id Protocol)
43: - **Mandate**: All Quadlets that mount host project directories MUST use `UserNS=keep-id` + `User=1000`. The `:U` flag is FORBIDDEN on shared host volumes.
44: - **Constraint**: Never use `:U` on volume mounts that the host user needs to access. Never use `:Z` or `:z` — they are SELinux flags, and Ubuntu uses AppArmor.
45: - **Pattern**: See `docs/research/R_PODMAN_SOVEREIGN_V2.md` for the verified Quadlet pattern.
46: - **Reason**: The `:U` flag destructively chowns host directories to UID 101000, locking the host user out. `UserNS=keep-id` maps host UID 1000 directly into the container — no chown needed.
47: - **IMPORTANT (D144)**: `UserNS=keep-id` + `User=1000` is the QUADLET-ONLY pattern. For `docker-compose` or `podman run`, OMIT `--user`/`user:` entirely — in rootless Podman, container UID 0 maps to host UID 1000 by default. Setting `user: "1000:1000"` maps to subuid 101000, breaking volume writes. Use `user:` only if also setting `userns_mode: keep-id` (incompatible with `--pod` in podman-compose v5.x). See PIVOT_LOG.md D144.
48: 
49: ### 7. Local-First (Non-Negotiable)
50: - **Mandate**: Local inference is PRIMARY. Cloud is FALLBACK. Always.

1: # 🔱 Grok CLI Architecture Study — Comprehensive Research Report
2: **AP Token**: `AP-GROK_CLI_RESEARCH-v1.0.0`
3: ⬡ OMEGA ⬡ ROC_RACOON ⬡ deepseek-v4-flash-free ⬡ opencode ⬡ trc_grok_research ⬡ COMPREHENSIVE
4: 
5: **Date**: 2026-07-17
6: **Session**: Post-compaction hydration → Grok CLI Phase 0 onboarding
7: **Handoff**: `ho_a1406ec69e74` (Researcher → Roc Racoon, ACCEPTED 20:10:39)
8: 
9: ---
10: 
11: ## 📋 Executive Summary
12: 
13: This report captures the complete architectural analysis of **Grok CLI (xai-org/grok-build)** — xAI's open-source terminal coding agent — as the reference implementation for the **Omega Engine TUI redesign**. All findings are verified against the cloned repository at `third_party/grok-build/` (85+ crates, Rust workspace) and official documentation, **supplemented by deep web research** on protocol specifications, ecosystem adoption, and implementation patterns.
14: 
15: **Key Insight**: Grok CLI uses a **pure Elm architecture** (Action → Dispatch → Effect → Event Loop) with **ACP (Agent Client Protocol)** as the UI↔Runtime boundary, **kernel-enforced sandboxing** via `nono` (Landlock/Seatbelt), and **JSONL session persistence** for crash resilience. These patterns map directly to Omega's sovereignty mandates.
16: 
17: ---
18: 
19: ## 🏗️ Core Architecture: The Elm Loop
20: 
21: ### Vocabulary (Source: `xai-grok-pager/src/app/actions.rs`)
22: 
23: | Concept | Type | Role |
24: |---------|------|------|
25: | **Action** | `enum Action` (2807 lines) | Synchronous, side-effect-free user intent. Produced by input handling, consumed by `dispatch()`. |
26: | **Effect** | `enum Effect` (1325-2807) | Async side effects produced by dispatch, executed by event loop. 1:1 maps to ACP calls. |
27: | **TaskResult** | `enum TaskResult` | Completion of spawned effects, fed back into dispatch. |
28: | **Dispatch** | `fn dispatch(action, app) -> Vec<Effect>` | Pure state transformer. NO I/O. Fully testable. |
29: 
30: ### Data Flow
31: ```
32: User Input → handle_input() → Action → dispatch() → (new_state, Vec<Effect>)
33:                                                               ↓
34: Event Loop (JoinSet) spawns Effects → TaskResult → dispatch() → ...
35: ```
36: 
37: **Critical Invariant**: `dispatch/` directory contains **pure reducers** — no terminal, network, or filesystem access.
38: 
39: ---
40: 
41: ## 🔗 Gap 1: `dispatch/` → `xai-grok-shell` Boundary (UI↔Runtime)
42: 
43: ### The Boundary: ACP over stdio JSON-RPC
44: 
45: | Layer | Crate | Responsibility |
46: |-------|-------|----------------|
47: | **UI (Pager)** | `xai-grok-pager` | Elm loop, rendering, input handling |
48: | **Runtime (Shell)** | `xai-grok-shell` | ACP server, session lifecycle, tool execution |
49: | **Bridge** | `xai-grok-pager/src/app/acp_handler/` | Routes ACP notifications to agents |
50: 
51: ### Key Files
52: - `xai-grok-pager/src/app/acp_handler/mod.rs` — Notification routing (709 lines)
53: - `xai-grok-shell/src/session/handle.rs` — SessionHandle with ACP command channel
54: - `xai-acp-lib/src/lib.rs` — Protocol definitions (`session/new`, `session/prompt`, `mcp/list`, etc.)
55: 
56: ### ACP Methods (from `agent_client_protocol` crate)
57: ```
58: session/new          → CreateSession
59: session/load         → LoadSession
60: session/prompt       → SendPrompt / SendPromptNow
61: session/cancel       → CancelTurn
62: session/set_model    → SwitchModel
63: session/set_mode     → SetSessionMode
64: x.ai/mcp/list        → FetchMcpsList
65: x.ai/mcp/auth_trigger → McpAuthTrigger
66: x.ai/hooks/*         → HooksAction
67: x.ai/queue/*         → QueueRemove/Reorder/Edit/Interject
68: x.ai/yolo_mode_changed → PersistPermissionMode
69: ```
70: 
71: ### Web Research: ACP Protocol Specification (2026)
72: 
73: **Official Spec**: https://agentclientprotocol.com | GitHub: `agentclientprotocol/agent-client-protocol`
74: 
75: | Aspect | Detail |
76: |--------|--------|
77: | **Transport** | JSON-RPC 2.0 over stdio (newline-delimited) |
78: | **Governance** | Jointly governed by Zed Industries + JetBrains (BDFL model), transitioning to independent foundation |
79: | **Complements** | MCP (Model Context Protocol) — ACP = editor↔agent, MCP = agent↔tools |
80: | **Protocol Version** | 1 (stable), unstable features in separate schema |
81: | **Initialization** | Mandatory `initialize` handshake before any session methods |
82: | **Agent Methods** | `initialize`, `session/new`, `session/load`, `session/prompt`, `session/cancel`, `session/set_model`, `session/set_mode`, `authenticate` |
83: | **Client Methods** | `fs/read_text_file`, `fs/write_text_file`, `session/request_permission`, `terminal/create`, `terminal/output`, `terminal/track_exit`, `terminal/terminate`, `terminal/release` |
84: | **Adoption (2026)** | Native: OpenCode, Cursor, Cline, Kilo, Qwen Code, Factory Droid, Augment Code. Bridge: Claude Code, Codex CLI, Gemini CLI, GitHub Copilot |
85: 
86: **ACP vs MCP Distinction** (from xAI blog & dev.to):
87: - **MCP**: Connects models to tools (databases, APIs, filesystems)
88: - **ACP**: Connects agents to other agents — multi-agent coordination, task delegation, sub-agent spawning
89: - **Bridge**: xAI released ACP-to-MCP adapter allowing Grok Build to call existing MCP servers
90: 
91: ### Omega Mapping
92: | Grok | Omega |
93: |------|-------|
94: | `xai-grok-shell` (ACP server) | `ModelGateway` + Provider Fabric |
95: | `Effect` enum | Our effect vocabulary for TUI |
96: | `SessionHandle` | Entity session management |
97: | `xai-acp-lib` | `omega-hub acp-server` for editor integration |
98: 
99: ---
100:

</content>

49: ## 1. Architecture Overview

51: ### 1.1 High-Level Design Philosophy
52: 
53: Grok Build achieves its polish through **rigorous separation of concerns**:
54: 
55: | Concern | Crate | Responsibility |
56: |---------|-------|----------------|

| Grok Build (`nono`) | Omega Engine (`ResourceGuard`) |

        "The design is closer to Claude Code (which also runs in the terminal) than to OpenAI Codex (which is desktop app and cloud-first). You install Grok Build, point it at a project directory, and interact with it through the terminal to plan, write, modify, and run code.\n\nHeadless scripting support means Grok Build can be invoked non-interactively - you pass a task as a command-line argument and it executes without requiring a TUI session. This enables Grok Build to run inside CI/CD pipelines, GitHub Actions workflows, cron jobs, and other automated contexts where a human is not present to interact with the TUI. Grok 4.\n3 is the model that introduced Skills - saved instruction sets that let the model remember your preferred code style, project conventions, and workflow patterns without re-explaining them every session. Skills carry into Grok Build sessions, meaning the agent learns and retains your preferences over time.\n\n## Agent Client Protocol (ACP) - What It Is and Why It Matters\n\nGrok Build launches with native Agent Client Protocol (ACP) support. ACP is a protocol specification for how AI agents communicate with each other and with orchestration systems - essentially a standard message format and capability declaration mechanism for multi-agent coordination. ACP is distinct from Anthropic's Model Context Protocol (MCP), which is primarily a standard for how AI systems connect to tools and data sources.\n\n|Protocol |Primary Purpose |Created By |Supported By |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\n|MCP (Model Context Protocol) |Agent-to-tool and agent-to-data-source connections |Anthropic |Claude Code, Cursor, most major AI tools (3,000+ servers) |\n|ACP (Agent Client Protocol) |Agent-to-agent communication and orchestration |IBM / Linux Foundation (open standard) |Grok Build, IBM Bee Agent, BeeAI framework |\n\nACP support means Grok Build can participate in multi-agent orchestration systems as a first-class agent - it can receive tasks from an orchestrator, report status, delegate subtasks to other ACP-compatible agents, and return structured results. In practice, this means Grok Build can be wired into enterprise agent pipelines alongside other ACP-compatible tools. MCP remains the dominant protocol for tool integrations in the AI agent ecosystem; ACP is earlier-stage but has IBM's backing and Linux Foundation governance behind it, which gives it credibility in enterprise contexts.\n\n## Grok Build vs Codex vs Claude Code"

        "[Home](https://www.scriptbyai.com/) [Free AI Tools](https://www.scriptbyai.com/tools/) [Programming](https://www.scriptbyai.com/tools/programming/) Grok Build: SpaceXAI’s Open-source Terminal AI Coding Agent\n\n# Grok Build: SpaceXAI’s Open-source Terminal AI Coding Agent\n\nInstall Grok Build and explore its terminal UI, sessions, Plan mode, worktrees, extensions, automation commands, and security controls.\n\ngrok-build-coding-cli\n\nGrok Build is SpaceXAI’s new open-source CLI AI coding agent with a full-screen TUI that reads the project, searches files, edits code, runs shell commands, checks web resources, and tracks work that continues beyond a single response.\n\nEach session retains its prompts, tool calls, and file snapshots, carrying the task from initial investigation through implementation, testing, and review.\n\nThe coding agent currently supports three operating models:\n\nInteractive sessions place the agent inside a mouse-aware terminal workspace.\nHeadless commands turn repository tasks into scripts or CI jobs with plain, JSON, or streaming output. Agent Client Protocol support connects the runtime to editors and other applications through standard input and output.\n\nGrok Build covers much more than prompt-driven file editing:\n\nPlan mode separates architectural decisions from code changes. Subagents divide large assignments into independent sessions. Git worktrees isolate parallel changes. Background commands, monitors, recurring prompts, and a live dashboard keep long-running work visible. Skills, plugins, hooks, MCP servers, project rules, and custom model endpoints turn the CLI into a configurable coding-agent platform.\n\n[Visit Grok Build](https://github.com/xai-org/grok-build)\n\n[Official Documentation](https://docs.x.ai/build/overview)\n\n## Key Features\n* **Full-screen terminal workspace:** The TUI combines the conversation, tool activity, diffs, tasks, subagents, session controls, model selection, and extension management in one terminal application.\n* **Repository-aware editing:** Grok Build reads directory structures, searches symbols and text, opens files, applies multi-file changes, runs commands, and inspects Git state from the current working directory.\n* **Plan mode:** A separate planning state keeps the proposed implementation visible while project file changes remain subject to approval.\n* **Subagent execution:** Independent child sessions handle research, implementation, review, and other task segments in parallel.\n* **Git worktree sessions:** Parallel sessions receive separate working directories and branches, protecting each agent’s file state from concurrent edits elsewhere."

        "The company released a Windows PowerShell installer for Grok Build, its terminal-native AI coding tool, on May 25, 2026, bringing plan-and-approve workflows, parallel subagents, and headless scripting to the platform that still dominates enterprise desktops.\n\nWhat Grok Build actually does\n\nGrok Build is a command-line interface that embeds xAI’s Grok models directly into a developer’s terminal environment.\n\nThe tool ships with several capabilities designed for professional software engineering teams. It supports an interactive terminal user interface (TUI), meaning developers can have structured back-and-forth sessions with the AI without leaving their terminal. There’s also a headless mode triggered by a -p flag, which lets teams run the agent in automated pipelines without human interaction.\n\nThe parallel subagent feature allows Grok Build to spin up multiple AI agents simultaneously to work on different parts of a codebase.\nPlan mode offers a structured workflow where the AI proposes changes, presents clean diffs showing exactly what it wants to modify, and waits for human approval before touching anything.\n\nThe tool also integrates with AGENTS.md documentation and existing repository configurations. xAI additionally introduced something called the ACP protocol for building custom bots on top of the platform.\n\nThe model under the hood\n\nGrok Build runs on the Grok Build 0.1 model, which xAI made available through its API on May 20, 2026, five days before the CLI launch. The model supports a context window of up to 256,000 tokens.\n\nAPI pricing sits at $1 per million input tokens and $2 per million output tokens.\n\nWindows users can install the tool by running a single PowerShell command: irm https://x.ai/cli/install.ps1 | iex.\n\nWho gets access, and who doesn’t\n\nThis is an early beta, and xAI is limiting access to SuperGrok and X Premium+ subscribers.\n\nDisclosure: This article was edited by Editorial Team.\nFor more information on how we create and review content, see our [Editorial Policy](https://cryptobriefing.com/editorial-policy/) .\n\n* ##### Tags:\n* [AI Agents](https://www.europesays.com/ai/tag/ai-agents/)\n* [Artificial Intelligence Agents](https://www.europesays.com/ai/tag/artificial-intelligence-agents/)\n\n[](https://www.facebook.com/sharer.php?u=https://www.europesays.com/ai/51250/)\n\n[](https://x.com/share?&text=xAI%20releases%20Grok%20CLI%20installer%20for%20Windows%20PowerShell%2C%20entering%20crowded%20AI%20coding%20agent%20race&url=https://www.europesays.com/ai/51250/)\n\n[](https://www.linkedin.com/shareArticle?mini=true&url=https://www.europesays.com/ai/51250/)\n\n[](http://www.reddit.com/submit?url=https://www.europesays.com/ai/51250/)\n\nAI Europe\n\n[www.europesays.com](https://www.europesays.com/)"

      "url": "https://deploybase.ai/articles/grok-deepsearch-vs-think",
      "title": "Grok DeepSearch vs Think Mode: Which to Use? | DeployBase",
      "publish_date": "2026-01-15",
      "excerpts": [
        "DeepSearch reduces hallucinations on questions where accurate information exists in web-accessible sources: product specifications, technical documentation, pricing information.\n\nBy grounding responses in actual published sources rather than pattern-based generation, DeepSearch reduces the risk of confidently-stated fabrications.\n\n### Information Recency Trade-offs\n\nWeb search integration introduces latency: retrieving and processing web results takes time, with typical DeepSearch queries requiring 5-15 seconds to return. This time investment trades for information currency measured in hours or days rather than months or years.\n\nFor applications where real-time information matters, this trade-off justifies the latency cost. For routine queries where information currency doesn't matter, the latency cost makes DeepSearch suboptimal.\n\n### Combining Modes for Maximum Accuracy\nSome applications benefit from combining DeepSearch and Think Mode: using DeepSearch to gather current information, then applying extended reasoning to analyze that information. This creates maximum accuracy for complex analytical tasks.\n\nFor example, a financial analysis system might use DeepSearch to retrieve current market data, then Think Mode to analyze that data and generate predictions. The combination uses both modes' strengths.\n\n## Latency Characteristics and Performance\n\n### DeepSearch Latency Profile\n\nDeepSearch latency scales with search result volume: simple queries requiring single-source verification generate quickly (5-10 seconds), while complex research questions requiring multiple sources and cross-referencing consume more time (15-30+ seconds).\n\nThe latency distribution remains unpredictable without testing on representative queries: a question appearing simple might require extensive searching, while complex questions might resolve with single targeted searches.\nFor interactive applications, this variability complicates SLA management. Teams should budget conservatively, assuming p99 latencies of 30+ seconds for production applications.\n\n### Think Mode Latency Profile\n\nThink Mode latency similarly scales with problem complexity: simple questions generate quickly while complex problems require extended thinking time. A straightforward factual question might complete in 3-5 seconds, while a challenging mathematical problem could require 15-30+ seconds.\n\nThe latency profile creates similar SLA management challenges as DeepSearch: unpredictable per-query timing without prior testing.\n\n### Combining Latency Considerations\n\nApplications using both DeepSearch and Think Mode face compounded latency: a query requiring both web search and extended reasoning might require 30-60+ seconds, far exceeding standard inference latency. Batch processing and asynchronous request handling become necessary"

        "* Home\n* Blog\n* Grok 4.5: xAI's (SpaceXAI) Game-Changing Leap in Coding, Agentic AI, and Cost-Efficient Frontier Intelligence\n\n# Grok 4.5: xAI's (SpaceXAI) Game-Changing Leap in Coding, Agentic AI, and Cost-Efficient Frontier Intelligence\n\n_2026-07-10_\n\n20251213221952\n\nMISSION BRIEF · SPACEXAI · 2026-07-08\n\n## GROK 4\\.5\n\nxAI's (SpaceXAI) Game-Changing Leap in Coding, Agentic AI, and Cost-Efficient Frontier Intelligence\n\nIn the rapidly evolving world of artificial intelligence, few releases have generated as much anticipation and immediate impact as Grok 4.5 from SpaceXAI (formerly xAI). Launched on July 8, 2026, this model isn't just another incremental upgrade — it's a purpose-built powerhouse optimized for real-world engineering, coding, agentic workflows, and knowledge work. Positioned as an \"Opus-class\" model but delivered at significantly faster speeds, higher token efficiency, and a fraction of the cost of competitors like Claude Opus 4.8 or GPT-5.5, Grok 4.\n5 is redefining what's possible for developers, businesses, and power users.\n\nWhether you're a software engineer debugging complex repositories, a data analyst automating multi-tool workflows, or an enterprise team seeking frontier-level performance without breaking the bank, Grok 4.5 delivers tangible advantages. This in-depth review covers its architecture, benchmarks, real-world capabilities, pricing, comparisons, use cases, limitations, and future outlook — backed by independent evaluations from Artificial Analysis and official announcements.\n\nParameters\n\n1\\.5T\n\nContext\n\n500K→1M\n\nSpeed\n\n80–86 TPS\n\nPrice In/Out\n\n$2 / $6\n\nGrok 4.5 overview\n\nSEC.01\n\n## The Road to Grok 4.5: From 4.3 to a 1.5T Parameter Beast\n\nSpaceXAI's journey with the Grok series has been marked by rapid iteration and a focus on maximum truth-seeking and utility. Grok 4.5 builds on the V9 foundation model — a massive 1.\n5 trillion parameter architecture, roughly three times larger than its predecessor's V8-small base. Trained on vast datasets spanning coding, science, engineering, and mathematics using tens of thousands of NVIDIA GB300 GPUs at the Memphis Colossus cluster, it received supplemental training with data from Cursor (a coding tool acquired by SpaceX).\n\nThis wasn't a general intelligence model retrofitted for tasks; it was engineered from the ground up for coding and agentic performance. sten guide on how to bring your custom Koog agent into IDE with native ACP × Koog integration](https://blog.jetbrains.com/ai/2026/02/koog-x-acp-connect-an-agent-to-your-ide-and-more/)\n\n[Explore the JetBrains AI Blog](https://blog.jetbrains.com/ai/tag/acp/)\n## Koog by JetBrains Build fault-tolerant, scalable, and enterprise-ready AI agents across all platforms – including JVM, Android, iOS, JavaScript, and WebAssembly.\n\n## FAQ\n\nWhat is the Agent Client Protocol (ACP)?\n\nThe Agent Client Protocol (ACP) is an open protocol that defines how IDEs and AI coding agents communicate. ACP makes it possible to use different agents across multiple editors without building custom integrations for each one.\n\nHow is ACP different from building IDE plugins?\n\nTraditional IDE plugins require separate, custom integrations for each editor–agent pair, which is time-consuming and fragile. ACP provides a shared communication protocol so editors and agents can interoperate without bespoke plugins, reducing overhead and lock-in.\n\nWhich IDEs support the Agent Client Protocol?\n\nACP support is growing. For the most up-to-date list of editors supporting ACP, please see the [ACP documentation](https://agentclientprotocol.com/get-started/clients) .\nWhich AI agents work with ACP?\n\nSimilar to IDE support, the list of agents supporting ACP is growing. Please see the [ACP documentation](https://agentclientprotocol.com/get-started/agents) for the most up-to-date list.\n\nCan I use my own or in-house AI agents with ACP?\n\nYes. Because ACP is a standard protocol, developers and teams can implement ACP in their own agents. Once an agent implements the ACP API, it can integrate with any ACP-supporting editor without relying on registry agents or vendor-specific solutions.\n\nDoes ACP lock me into a specific IDE or AI agent?\n\nNo – one of ACP's core principles is no vendor lock-in. Agents that implement ACP can be used in any editor that supports ACP, and editors supporting ACP can connect to any ACP-compatible agent, giving developers freedom to mix and match.\n\nHow do I build an AI agent that works with multiple IDEs?\n\nTo build an agent that works across multiple editors, check out the [ACP documentation](https://agentclientprotocol.\ncom/protocol/overview) .\n\nWhat's the difference between ACP (Agent Client Protocol) and LSP (Language Server Protocol)?\n\nBoth protocols standardize communication, but for different layers:\n\n* LSP (Language Server Protocol) standardizes how editors and language servers communicate language-specific features.\n* ACP standardizes how editors or IDEs and AI coding agents communicate. ACP is an interoperability layer for agent workflows, similar in idea to what LSP did for language features.\n\nJetBrains is not affiliated with or sponsored by Dynalist Inc."

        "# tracking(serve): ACP Streamable HTTP transport — implementation status, RFD alignment & upgrade plan\n\n- Page: GitHub issue\n- URL: https://github.com/QwenLM/qwen-code/issues/4782\n- State: open\n- Author: chiga0\n- Created: 2026-06-04T13:29:43Z\n- Updated: 2026-07-15T06:35:39Z\n- Repository: QwenLM/qwen-code\n- Number: #4782\n- Comments: 2\n- Assignees: chiga0\n- Milestone: v1.x\n\n## Labels\n\n- category/core\n- daemon\n- scope/non-interactive\n- status/needs-triage\n- type/feature-request\n\n---\n\n## TL;DR\n\nQwen-Code Daemon now implements the [ACP (Agent Client Protocol)](https://agentclientprotocol.com) Streamable HTTP transport at \\`/acp\\`. This means ACP-native editors like **Zed**, **Goose**, and **JetBrains** can connect to \\`qwen serve\\` **without any adapter code** — just point them at \\`http://host:4170/acp\\`.\n\nThis issue tracks the implementation status, documents how we align with the official ACP RFD, and defines how we keep up with protocol evolution.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Why We Built \\`/acp\\`\n\n### The Problem\nQwen-Code Daemon originally exposed a **bespoke REST + SSE API** (\\`POST /session/:id/prompt\\`, \\`GET /session/:id/events\\`, etc.). This works, but:\n\n- Every new client (IDE plugin, Web UI, IM bot) must learn and implement our specific HTTP contract\n- No existing editor can talk to us out-of-the-box\n- We maintain our own wire format, error conventions, and capability negotiation — duplicating work the industry is standardizing\n\n### The Solution\n\nThe [Agent Client Protocol (ACP)](https://agentclientprotocol.com) is an open standard for communication between code editors and AI coding agents. It is backed by **Zed** (creator), **Google** (Gemini CLI), **JetBrains**, and a growing community. ACP defines:\n\n- A set of **JSON-RPC 2.0 methods** for session management, prompting, permissions, and configuration\n- A **transport layer** (stdio for local, HTTP Streamable + WebSocket for remote)\n- An **extensibility model** (\\`_meta\\` fields + underscore-prefixed vendor methods)\n\nBy implementing ACP's HTTP Streamable transport at \\`/acp\\`, we get:\n| Benefit | Details |\n|---------|---------|\n| **Zero-glue integration** | Zed, Goose, JetBrains can connect directly — no adapter, no plugin, no SDK |\n| **Standard protocol** | Third-party tools speak ACP; they speak to us automatically |\n| **Dual transport** | Existing REST API stays untouched; \\`/acp\\` is additive |\n| **Ecosystem leverage** | ACP's SDKs (TypeScript, Python, Java, Kotlin, Rust) work with our endpoint |\n\n### What We Did NOT Do\n\n- We did **not remove** the existing REST API — it stays for backward compatibility\n- We did **not fork** the protocol — we implement the standard + vendor extensions under \\`_qwen/*\\`\n- We did **not block** on the RFD being \"stable\" — the transport RFD is in Phase 1, but the protocol (methods, types) is stable and in production use by Gemini CLI and Zed\n\n---\n\n## 2. Architecture\n\n\\`\\`\\`\n                    ┌─ REST surface ──── /session/*, /workspace/*, /file/*\nqwen serve ────────┤\n\n...\n\n| **Cookie support** | Not implemented | None | Our trust model is bearer-token, not session-cookie; daemon is single-user |\n| **SSE resumability** (\\`Last-Event-ID\\`) | Not implemented on \\`/acp\\` | Low | RFD marks as Phase 4 (future). Our REST SSE already has resumability via EventBus ring buffer; \\`/acp\\` SSE can add it later |\n| **\\`unstable_forkSession\\`** | Not implemented | Low | Marked UNSTABLE in SDK; no current consumer |\n| **\\`logout\\` method** | Not implemented | Low | Bearer token revocation is out-of-scope for local daemon |\n\n### SDK Version Gap\n\n| | Current | Latest |\n|---|---------|--------|\n| \\`@agentclientprotocol/sdk\\` | **0.14.1** | **0.21.0** |\n\nSee #4227 for the upgrade tracking. Key unlocks:\n- \\`session/close\\` stabilized as standard method\n- \\`session/resume\\` stabilized\n- \\`session/list\\` stabilized\n- New types for conformance checking\n\n---\n\n## 5. Can a Zed / Goose / Third-Party Agent Client Connect Today?\n\n**Yes**, once PRs #4563 → #4736 → #4737 merge.         "# agentclientprotocol/agent-client-protocol\n\n- Page: GitHub repository\n- URL: https://github.com/agentclientprotocol/agent-client-protocol\n- Description:  A protocol for connecting any editor to any agent - agentclientprotocol/agent-client-protocol\n- Stars: 3,691\n- Forks: 306\n- License: Apache-2.0 license\n- Default branch: main\n- Created: 2025-06-23T17:39:36.000Z\n- Commits: 1,825\n\n## Top-level files\n\n- .github/\n- agent-client-protocol-schema/\n- docs/\n- schema-generator/\n- schema/\n- scripts/\n- .gitignore\n- .prettierignore\n- .prettierrc\n- .release-plz.toml\n- AGENTS.md\n- CHANGELOG.md\n- CLAUDE.md\n- CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md\n- CONTRIBUTING.md\n- Cargo.lock\n- Cargo.toml\n- GOVERNANCE.md\n- LICENSE\n- MAINTAINERS.md\n- README.md\n- clippy.toml\n- deny.toml\n- package-lock.json\n- package.json\n- typos.toml\n\n## README.md\n\n[Agent Client Protocol](https://agentclientprotocol.com/)\n\n# Agent Client Protocol\nThe Agent Client Protocol (ACP) standardizes communication between _code editors_ (interactive programs for viewing and editing source code) and _coding agents_ (programs that use generative AI to autonomously modify code).\n\nLearn more at [agentclientprotocol.com](https://agentclientprotocol.com/) .\n\n## Rust Crate and Schema Artifacts\n\nThis repository's root Rust crate is [`agent-client-protocol-schema`](https://crates.io/crates/agent-client-protocol-schema) . It provides the Rust data model for ACP wire messages, including request, response, notification, JSON-RPC envelope, and protocol-version types. Use this crate when you need direct access to ACP protocol types, schema-oriented tooling, or code generation inputs.\n\nIf you are implementing a Rust ACP agent or client, start with the higher-level [`agent-client-protocol`](https://crates.io/crates/agent-client-protocol) runtime crate instead.\nThat crate provides the client and agent runtime APIs for ACP integrations; this schema crate is the lower-level protocol type surface.\n\nGenerated JSON Schema artifacts live in [`schema/v1`](https://github.com/agentclientprotocol/agent-client-protocol/blob/main/schema/v1) and [`schema/v2`](https://github.com/agentclientprotocol/agent-client-protocol/blob/main/schema/v2) . When a schema release is published, the versioned `.json` files are also attached to the corresponding [`schema-v*` GitHub release](https://github.com/agentclientprotocol/agent-client-protocol/releases) , which is the recommended download surface for SDK generators and other release automation.\n\n## Versioning\n\nThe Rust crate version and JSON Schema release versions describe the Rust crate and JSON Schema artifacts themselves. The Rust crate is published to crates.io, while the versioned JSON Schema files are attached to GitHub releases rather than published as crates.\nThese versions follow the compatibility expectations of those artifacts: Rust APIs, generated schema structure, artifact layout, and other details that downstream SDKs or code generators may consume.\n\n**The current stable ACP protocol version is `1` .**\n\nACP wire compatibility is determined separately by the protocol version exchanged during `initialize` via `protocolVersion` . The `version` field in the versioned `schema/*/meta*.json` files also describes the ACP protocol version that the corresponding schema represents.\n\nThis means two versions of the JSON Schema artifacts can describe the same wire-compatible ACP protocol version while having different schema structure for SDK generators. For example, a release might change how definitions are organized, named, or emitted in the JSON Schema in a way that affects downstream code generation without changing the JSON messages exchanged by ACP clients and agents.\nConsumers should not infer wire compatibility from the crate or schema release version alone. Use the negotiated `protocolVersion` to determine the ACP wire protocol shape and breaking-compatibility level. Within a protocol version, use the exchanged capabilities to decide which optional ACP messages and features are supported. Use artifact versions to manage compatibility with this repository's Rust and schema outputs.\n\n## Integrations\n\n* [Schema](https://github.com/agentclientprotocol/agent-client-protocol/blob/main/schema/v1/schema.json)\n* [Agents](https://agentclientprotocol.com/overview/agents)\n* [Clients](https://agentclientprotocol.com/overview/clients)\n* Official Libraries\n  \n    + **Kotlin** : [`acp-kotlin`](https://github.com/agentclientprotocol/kotlin-sdk) \\- Supports JVM, other targets are in progress, see [samples](https://github.com/agentclientprotocol/kotlin-sdk/tree/master/samples"
      ]

        "SUN, JULY 19, 2026\n\n | [Join Us ★](https://aitoolsrecap.com/JoinUs.aspx?package=free)\n\nIndependent · In‑Depth · Practitioner‑Tested\n\nThe Journal of Artificial Intelligence Tools\n\n Join Us ★\n\n✎ General\n\n# xAI Grok Build Is Now in Beta - Here's How It Stacks Up Against Codex and Claude Code\n\nxAI launched Grok Build beta in early June 2026 - a terminal TUI coding agent on Grok 4.3 with headless scripting for CI pipelines and native Agent Client Protocol (ACP) support for multi-agent orchestration. Key differentiator: native X/Twitter real-time data. Key gaps: beta status, no desktop computer use, smaller MCP ecosystem than Claude Code. Available on SuperGrok ($30/mo) and X Premium+.\n\nBy AIToolsRecap June 2, 2026 7 min read 5081 views\n\nHome › Articles › [General](https://aitoolsrecap.com/Articles.aspx?category=code-tools) › [Grok](https://aitoolsrecap.com/Articles.aspx?product=grok) › xAI Grok Build Beta 2026: What It Is, Agent Cli...\n\n...\n\nThe design is closer to Claude Code (which also runs in the terminal) than to OpenAI Codex (which is desktop app and cloud-first). You install Grok Build, point it at a project directory, and interact with it through the terminal to plan, write, modify, and run code.\n\nHeadless scripting support means Grok Build can be invoked non-interactively - you pass a task as a command-line argument and it executes without requiring a TUI session. This enables Grok Build to run inside CI/CD pipelines, GitHub Actions workflows, cron jobs, and other automated contexts where a human is not present to interact with the TUI. This is the \"while-I-sleep\" automation use case that has driven developer interest in all three coding agents.\n\nThe agent runs on Grok 4.3 - the current xAI flagship model, stable release as of April 17, 2026. Grok 4.\n3 is the model that introduced Skills - saved instruction sets that let the model remember your preferred code style, project conventions, and workflow patterns without re-explaining them every session. Skills carry into Grok Build sessions, meaning the agent learns and retains your preferences over time.\n\n## Agent Client Protocol (ACP) - What It Is and Why It Matters\n\nGrok Build launches with native Agent Client Protocol (ACP) support. ACP is a protocol specification for how AI agents communicate with each other and with orchestration systems - essentially a standard message format and capability declaration mechanism for multi-agent coordination. ACP is distinct from Anthropic's Model Context Protocol (MCP), which is primarily a standard for how AI systems connect to tools and data sources.\n\n|Protocol |Primary Purpose |Created By |Supported By |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\n|MCP (Model Context Protocol) |Agent-to-tool and agent-to-data-source connections |Anthropic |Claude Code, Cursor, most major AI tools (3,000+ servers) |\n|ACP (Agent Client Protocol) |Agent-to-agent communication and orchestration |IBM / Linux Foundation (open standard) |Grok Build, IBM Bee Agent, BeeAI framework |\n\nACP support means Grok Build can participate in multi-agent orchestration systems as a first-class agent - it can receive tasks from an orchestrator, report status, delegate subtasks to other ACP-compatible agents, and return structured results. In practice, this means Grok Build can be wired into enterprise agent pipelines alongside other ACP-compatible tools. MCP remains the dominant protocol for tool integrations in the AI agent ecosystem; ACP is earlier-stage but has IBM's backing and Linux Foundation governance behind it, which gives it credibility in enterprise contexts.\n\n## Grok Build vs Codex vs Claude Code\n|Feature |Grok Build (xAI) |Codex (OpenAI) |Claude Code (Anthropic) |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\n|Interface |Terminal TUI |Desktop app + CLI + mobile |Terminal CLI |\n|Headless / CI support |Yes |Yes |Yes |\n|Real-time data access |X/Twitter + web (native) |Web search (tool) |Web search (tool) |\n|Context window |256K tokens (Grok 4.3) |128K (272K configurable) |1M tokens (beta) |\n|Agent protocol |ACP (IBM/Linux Foundation) |OpenAI tool spec |MCP (3,000+ servers) |\n|Desktop computer use |No |Yes (Windows + Mac) |Via Cowork |\n|Async / fire-and-forget |Via headless scripting |Yes (core design) |Via Managed Agents (enterprise) |\n|Pricing entry point |SuperGrok $30/mo |ChatGPT Plus $20/mo |Claude Pro $20/mo |\n|Status |Beta |GA |GA |\n\nGrok Build's genuine differentiator is native X/Twitter data access. The Grok 4.3 model has direct access to X's full firehose in real time - not web search that occasionally surfaces X posts, but actual real-time access to the platfor"

        "#### Tools\n\n# Overview\n\nThe xAI API supports **tool calling**, enabling Grok to perform actions beyond generating text—like searching the web, executing code, querying your data, or calling your own custom functions. Tools extend what's possible with the API and let you build powerful, interactive applications.\n\n## Types of Tools\n\nThe xAI API offers two categories of tools:\n\n| Type | Description | Examples |\n|------|-------------|----------|\n| **Built-in Tools** | Server-side tools managed by xAI that execute automatically | Web Search, X Search, Code Interpreter, Collections Search |\n| **Function Calling** | Custom functions you define that the model can invoke | Database queries, API calls, custom business logic |\n\nBuilt-in tools run on xAI's servers—you provide the tool configuration, and the API handles execution and returns results. Function calling lets you define your own tools that the model can request, giving you full control over what happens when they're invoked.\n## Pricing\n\nTool requests are priced based on two components: **token usage** and **tool invocations**. Since the model may call multiple tools to answer a query, costs scale with complexity.\n\nFor more details on Tools pricing, please check out [the pricing page](/developers/pricing#tools-pricing).\n\n## How It Works\n\nWhen you provide tools to a request, the xAI API can use them to gather information or perform actions:\n\n1. **Analyzes the query** and determines what information or actions are needed\n2. **Decides what to do next**: Make a tool call, or provide a final answer\n3. **Executes the tool** (for built-in tools) or returns a tool call request (for function calling)\n4. **Processes results** and continues until sufficient information is gathered\n5. **Returns the final response** with citations where applicable\n\n## Quick Start\n\n```bash customLanguage=\"bash\"\ncurl https://api.x.ai/v1/responses \\\n  -H \"Content-Type: application/json\" \\\n  -H \"Authorization: Bearer $XAI_API_KEY\" \\\n\n...\n\nresponses.create(\n    model=\"grok-4.5\",\n    input=[\n        {\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": \"What are the latest updates from xAI?\"}\n    ],\n    tools=[\n        {\"type\": \"web_search\"},\n        {\"type\": \"x_search\"},\n        {\"type\": \"code_interpreter\"},\n    ],\n    stream=True,\n)\n\nfor event in response:\n    if event.type == \"response.output_text.delta\":\n        print(event.delta, end=\"\", flush=True)\n```\n\n```javascriptAISDK\nimport { xai } from '@ai-sdk/xai';\nimport { streamText } from 'ai';\n\nconst { fullStream } = streamText({\n  model: xai.responses('grok-4.5'),\n  prompt: 'What are the latest updates from xAI?',\n  tools: {\n    web_search: xai.tools.webSearch(),\n    x_search: xai.tools.xSearch(),\n    code_execution: xai.tools.codeExecution(),\n  },\n});\n\nfor await (const part of fullStream) {\n  if (part.type === 'text-delta') {\n    process.stdout.write(part.text);\n  } else if (part.type === 'source' && part.sourceType === 'url') {\n    console.log(`Citation: ${part.url}`);\n  }\n}\n```\n```javascriptOpenAISDK\nimport OpenAI from \"openai\";\n\nconst client = new OpenAI({\n  apiKey: process.env.XAI_API_KEY,\n  baseURL: \"https://api.x.ai/v1\",\n});\n\nconst stream = await client.responses.create({\n  model: \"grok-4.5\",\n  input: [\n    { role: \"user\", content: \"What are the latest updates from xAI?\" }\n  ],\n  tools: [\n    { type: \"web_search\" },\n    { type: \"x_search\" },\n    { type: \"code_interpreter\" },\n  ],\n  stream: true,\n});\n\nfor await (const event of stream) {\n  if (event.type === \"response.output_text.delta\") {\n    process.stdout.write(event.delta);\n  }\n}\n```\n\n## Citations\n\nThe API automatically returns source URLs for information gathered via tools. See [Citations](/developers/tools/citations) for details on accessing and using citation data.\n\n## Next Steps\n\n* **[Function Calling](/developers/tools/function-calling)** - Define custom tools the model can call\n* **[Web Search](/developers/tools/web-search)** - Search the web and browse pages\n* **[X Search](/developers/tools/x-search)** - Search X posts, users, and threads\n* **[Code Execution](/developers/tools/code-execution)** - Execute Python code in a sandbox\n* **[Collections Search](/developers/tools/collections-search)** - Query your uploaded documents\n* **[Citations](/developers/tools/citations)** - Access source URLs and inline citations"

        "#### Quickstart\n\n# Quickstart\n\nWelcome! The same model powers agentic coding in Grok Build and is available on the API in early access:\n\n```bash\ncurl https://api.x.ai/v1/responses \\\n  -H \"Authorization: Bearer $XAI_API_KEY\" \\\n  -H \"Content-Type: application/json\" \\\n  -d '{\n    \"model\": \"grok-4.5\",\n    \"input\": \"Fix this function and explain the bug: function median(a){a.sort();return a[a.length/2]}\"\n  }'\n```\n\n```python customLanguage=\"pythonXAI\"\nimport os\nfrom xai_sdk import Client\nfrom xai_sdk.chat import user\n\nclient = Client(api_key=os.getenv(\"XAI_API_KEY\"))\n\nchat = a[a.length/2]}\"))\n\nprint(chat.sample().content)\n```\n\n```python customLanguage=\"pythonOpenAISDK\"\nfrom openai import OpenAI\n\nclient = OpenAI(\n    api_key=\"<YOUR_XAI_API_KEY_HERE>\",\n    base_url=\"https://api.x.\nai/v1\",\n)\n\nresponse = client.responses.create(\n    model=\"grok-4.5\",\n    input=\"Fix this function and explain the bug: function median(a){a.sort();return         "#### Model Capabilities\n\n# Imagine Overview\n\nThe Imagine API lets you generate and edit images and videos with Grok Imagine models. Use it for image generation, image editing with up to 3 reference images, video generation from text or still images, video editing, and more.\n\n## Pricing\n\nImage generation uses flat per-image pricing regardless of prompt length. Each generated image incurs a fixed fee. Image edits are billed for both the input image and the generated output image. Video generation uses per-second pricing where both duration and resolution affect the total cost. For full pricing details, see the [pricing page](/developers/pricing#imagine-api-pricing).\n\n## Image Generation\n\nGenerate new images from text prompts with Grok Imagine models. Configure output count (up to 10 images per request), aspect ratio, resolution, and response format.\n\n```python customLanguage=\"pythonXAI\"\nimport xai_sdk\n\nclient = xai_sdk.Client()\n\nresponse = client.image.sample(\nprompt=\"A collage of London landmarks in a stenciled street‑art style\",\n    model=\"grok-imagine-image-quality\",\n)\n\nprint(response.url)\n```\n\n```bash\ncurl -X POST https://api.x.ai/v1/images/generations \\\n  -H \"Content-Type: application/json\" \\\n  -H \"Authorization: Bearer $XAI_API_KEY\" \\\n  -d '{\n    \"model\": \"grok-imagine-image-quality\",\n    \"prompt\": \"A collage of London landmarks in a stenciled street‑art style\"\n  }'\n```\n\n```python customLanguage=\"pythonOpenAISDK\"\nfrom openai import OpenAI\n\nclient = OpenAI(\n    base_url=\"https://api.x.ai/v1\",\n    api_key=\"YOUR_API_KEY\",\n)\n\nresponse = client.images.generate(\n    model=\"grok-imagine-image-quality\",\n    prompt=\"A collage of London landmarks in a stenciled street‑art style\",\n)\n\nprint(response.data[0].url)\n```\n\n```javascript customLanguage=\"javascriptAISDK\"\nimport { xai } from \"@ai-sdk/xai\";\nimport { generateImage } from \"ai\";\n\nconst { image } = await generateImage({\n    model: xai.image(\"grok-imagine-image-quality\"),\nprompt: \"A collage of London landmarks in a stenciled street‑art style\",\n});\n\nconsole.log(image.base64);\n```\n\n## Image Editing\n\nEdit a source image with natural language. Provide a public image URL or base64-encoded data URI, then describe the change you want Grok Imagine to apply. Multi-image editing supports up to 3 source images in a single request for combining subjects, transferring styles, and composing scenes.\n\n```python customLanguage=\"pythonXAI\"\nimport base64\nimport xai_sdk\n\nclient = xai_sdk.Client()\n\n# Load image from file and encode as base64\nwith open(\"photo.png\", \"rb\") as f:\n    image_data = base64.b64encode(f.read()).decode(\"utf-8\")\n\nresponse = client.image.sample(\n    prompt=\"Render this as a pencil sketch with detailed shading\",\n    model=\"grok-imagine-image-quality\",\n    image_url=f\"data:image/png;base64,{image_data}\",\n)\n\nprint(response.url)\n```\n\n```bash\n# Using a public URL as the source image\ncurl -X POST https://api.x.ai/v1/images/edits \\\n-H \"Content-Type: application/json\" \\\n  -H \"Authorization: Bearer $XAI_API_KEY\" \\\n  -d '{\n    \"model\": \"grok-imagine-image-quality\",\n    \"prompt\": \"Render this as a pencil sketch with detailed shading\",\n    \"image\": {\n      \"url\": \"https://docs.x.ai/assets/api-examples/images/style-realistic.png\",\n      \"type\": \"image_url\"\n    }\n  }'\n```\n\n```javascript customLanguage=\"javascriptAISDK\"\nimport { xai } from \"@ai-sdk/xai\";\nimport { generateImage } from \"ai\";\nimport fs from \"fs\";\n\n// Load image and encode as base64\nconst imageBuffer = fs.readFileSync(\"photo.png\");\nconst base64Image = imageBuffer.toString(\"base64\");\n\nconst { image } = await generateImage({\n    model: xai.image(\"grok-imagine-image-quality\"),\n    prompt: {\n        text: \"Render this as a pencil sketch with detailed shading\",\n        images: [`data:image/png;base64,${base64Image}`],\n    },\n});\n\nconsole.log(image.base64);\n```\n\n## Image-to-Video\n\nAnimate a still image with a text prompt.\nThe source image becomes the starting point for the generated video. Video requests are asynchronous: start a request, poll with the returned request ID, and use the completed video URL when ready. The xAI SDK and AI SDK handle polling for you.\n\n```python customLanguage=\"pythonXAI\"\nimport os\nimport xai_sdk\n\nclient = xai_sdk.Client(api_key=os.getenv(\"XAI_API_KEY\"))\n\nresponse = client.video.generate(\n    prompt=\"Make the water crash down and slowly pan out the camera\",\n    model=\"grok-imagine-video-1.5\",\n    image_url=\"https://docs.x.ai/assets/api-examples/video/waterfall-still.png\",\n    duration=12,\n)\n\nprint(response.url)\n```\n\n```javascript customLanguage=\"javascriptAISDK\"\nimport { xai } from \"@ai-sdk/xai\";\nimport { experimental_generateVideo as generateVideo } from \"ai\";\n\nconst result = await generateVideo({\n    model: xai.video(\"grok-imagine-video-1.5\"),\n    prompt: {\n        image: \"https://docs.x.ai/assets/api-examples/video/waterfall-still.png\","
      ]

      "url": "https://docs.x.ai/developers/tools/web-search",
      "title": "Web Search | xAI Docs",
      "publish_date": "2026-05-27",
      "excerpts": [
        "#### Tools\n\n# Web Search\n\nThe Web Search tool enables Grok to search the web in real-time and browse web pages to find information. This powerful tool allows the model to search the internet, access web pages, and extract relevant information to answer queries with up-to-date content.\n\n## SDK Support\n\n| SDK/API | Tool Name |\n|---------|-----------|\n| xAI SDK | `web_search` |\n| OpenAI Responses API | `web_search` |\n| Vercel AI SDK | `xai.tools.webSearch()` |\n\nThis tool is also supported in all Responses API compatible SDKs.\n\n## Basic Usage\n\n```pythonXAI\nimport os\n\nfrom xai_sdk import Client\nfrom xai_sdk.chat import user\nfrom xai_sdk.tools import web_search\n\nclient = Client(api_key=os.getenv(\"XAI_API_KEY\"))\nchat = client.chat.create(\n    model=\"grok-4.5\",  # reasoning model\n    tools=[web_search()],\n    include=[\"verbose_streaming\"],\n)\n\nchat.append(user(\"What is xAI?\"))\n\nis_thinking = True\nfor response, chunk in chat.stream():\n    for tool_call in chunk.tool_calls:\n\n...\n\nimport { generateText } from 'ai';\n\nconst { text, sources } = await generateText({\n  model: xai.responses('grok-4.5'),\n  prompt: 'What is xAI?',\n  tools: {\n    web_search: xai.tools.webSearch(),\n  },\n});\n\nconsole.log(text);\nconsole.log('Citations:', sources);\n```\n\n```bash\ncurl https://api.x.ai/v1/responses \\\\\n  -H \"Content-Type: application/json\" \\\\\n  -H \"Authorization: Bearer $XAI_API_KEY\" \\\\\n  -d '{\n  \"model\": \"grok-4.5\",\n  \"input\": [\n    {\n      \"role\": \"user\",\n      \"content\": \"What is xAI?\"\n    }\n  ],\n  \"tools\": [\n    {\n      \"type\": \"web_search\"\n    }\n  ]\n}'\n```\n\n## Web Search Parameters\n\n| Parameter | Description |\n|-----------|-------------|\n| `allowed_domains` | Only search within specific domains (max 5) |\n| `excluded_domains` | Exclude specific domains from search (max 5) |\n| `enable_image_understanding` | Enable analysis of images found during browsing |\n| `enable_image_search` | Enable image search results that can be embedded in responses |\n\n...\n\n\"enable_image_understanding\": True,\n        },\n    ],\n)\n```\n\n```javascriptAISDK\nconst { text } = await generateText({\n  model: xai.responses('grok-4.5'),\n  prompt: \"What is included in the image in xAI's official website?\",\n  tools: {\n    web_search: xai.tools.webSearch({\n      enableImageUnderstanding: true,\n    }),\n  },\n});\n```\n\n### Enable Image Search\n\nSetting `enable_image_search` to true lets Grok search for relevant images and include them in the response as Markdown image embeds such as `![alt](url)`.\n\n> [!NOTE]\n>\n> After Grok searches for images, the returned images are included in the model context used to write the response. This is separate from `enable_image_understanding`, which lets Grok inspect images it finds while browsing regular web pages.\n\nThe Vercel AI SDK does not yet expose `enableImageSearch`; the examples below use the Responses API and xAI Python SDK.\n\n```bash customLanguage=\"bash\"\ncurl https://api.x.ai/v1/responses \\\n-H \"Content-Type: application/json\" \\\n  -H \"Authorization: Bearer $XAI_API_KEY\" \\\n  -d '{\n  \"model\": \"grok-4.5\",\n  \"input\": [\n    {\n      \"role\": \"user\",\n      \"content\": \"Show me images of Starship on the launch pad.\"\n    }\n  ],\n  \"tools\": [\n    {\n      \"type\": \"web_search\",\n      \"enable_image_search\": true\n    }\n  ]\n}'\n```\n\n```python customLanguage=\"pythonXAI\"\nimport os\n\nfrom xai_sdk import Client\nfrom xai_sdk.chat import user\nfrom xai_sdk.tools import web_search\n\nclient = Client(api_key=os.getenv(\"XAI_API_KEY\"))\nchat = client.chat.create(\n    model=\"grok-4.5\",\n    tools=[\n        web_search(enable_image_search=True),\n    ],\n)\n\nchat.append(user(\"Show me images of Starship on the launch pad.\"))\nresponse = chat.sample()\nprint(response.content)\nprint(response.server_side_tool_usage)\n```\n\n```python customLanguage=\"pythonOpenAISDK\"\nresponse = client.responses.create(\n    model=\"grok-4.5\",\n    input=[\n        {\n            \"role\": \"user\",\n\"content\": \"Show me images of Starship on the launch pad.\",\n        },\n    ],\n    tools=[\n        {\n            \"type\": \"web_search\",\n            \"enable_image_search\": True,\n        },\n    ],\n)\n\nprint(response)\n```\n\nA response can include Markdown image embeds directly in the output text:\n\n```output\n![Why the SpaceX Starship launch pad matters](https://www.astronomy.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/09/starship-test-flight-mission-scaled.jpg)\n\nHere are several high-quality images of SpaceX's Starship on the launch pad at Starbase in Boca Chica, Texas.\n```\n\nIn the xAI SDK, successful image search executions appear in `response.server_side_tool_usage` as `SERVER_SIDE_TOOL_IMAGE_SEARCH`.\n\n## Citations\n\nFor details on how to retrieve and use citations from search results, see the [Citations](/developers/tools/citations) page."
      ]

        "Docs\n\nSearch\n\nCtrl+ K\n\n[API Console](https://console.x.ai)\n\nProducts\n\n[Grok](https://grok.com/?referrer=docs)\n\nResources\n\nllms.txt [Discord](https://discord.gg/x-ai) Email support [Terms and Policies](https://x.ai/legal)\n\n#### Model Capabilities # Imagine Overview Copy for LLM View as Markdown\n\n[Create API key](https://console.x.ai/team/default/api-keys)\n\nThe Imagine API lets you generate and edit images and videos with Grok Imagine models. Use it for image generation, image editing with up to 3 reference images, video generation from text or still images, video editing, and more.\n\n### Image Editing\n\nEdit images with natural language. Supports up to 3 reference images per request.\n\nPricing $0.02 / image\n\nResolution 1K & 2K\n\nRead docs [Try in playground](https://console.x.ai/playground/imagine)\n\n### Image Generation\n\nGenerate images from text prompts with configurable aspect ratio, resolution, and count.\n\nPricing $0.02 / image\n\nResolution 1K & 2K\nRead docs [Try in playground](https://console.x.ai/playground/imagine)\n\n### Image-to-Video\n\nAnimate a still image with a text prompt. The source image becomes the first frame.\n\nPricing $0.05 / sec\n\nResolution 480p & 720p\n\nRead docs [Try in playground](https://console.x.ai/playground/imagine)\n\n## Pricing\n\nImage generation uses flat per-image pricing regardless of prompt length. Each generated image incurs a fixed fee. Image edits are billed for both the input image and the generated output image. Video generation uses per-second pricing where both duration and resolution affect the total cost. For full pricing details, see the models page .\n\n## Image Generation\n\nGenerate new images from text prompts with Grok Imagine models. Configure output count (up to 10 images per request), aspect ratio, resolution, and response format.\n\nView docs Python Bash Python (OpenAI) JavaScript\n\n```\nimport  xai_sdk client  =  xai_sdk.Client() response  =  client.image.\nsample(     prompt = \"A collage of London landmarks in a stenciled street‑art style\" ,     model = \"grok-imagine-image-quality\" , ) print (response.url)\n```\n\n```\ncurl  -X  POST  https://api.x.ai/v1/images/generations  \\   -H  \"Content-Type: application/json\"  \\   -H  \"Authorization: Bearer  $XAI_API_KEY \"  \\   -d  '{     \"model\": \"grok-imagine-image-quality\",     \"prompt\": \"A collage of London landmarks in a stenciled street‑art style\"   }'\n```\n\n```\nfrom  openai  import  OpenAI client  =  OpenAI(     base_url = \"https://api.x.ai/v1\" ,     api_key = \"YOUR_API_KEY\" , ) response  =  client.images.generate(     model = \"grok-imagine-image-quality\" ,     prompt = \"A collage of London landmarks in a stenciled street‑art style\" , ) print (response.data[ 0 ].url)\n```\n\n```\nimport  { xai }  from  \"@ai-sdk/xai\" ; import  { generateImage }  from  \"ai\" ; const  {  image  }  =  await  generateImage ({     model: xai.\n\n...\n\n# Using a public URL as the source image curl  -X  POST  https://api.x.ai/v1/images/edits  \\   -H  \"Content-Type: application/json\"  \\   -H  \"Authorization: Bearer  $XAI_API_KEY \"  \\   -d  '{     \"model\": \"grok-imagine-image-quality\",     \"prompt\": \"Render this as a pencil sketch with detailed shading\",     \"image\": {       \"url\": \"https://docs.x.ai/assets/api-examples/images/style-realistic.png\",       \"type\": \"image_url\"     }   }'\n```\n\n```\nimport  { xai }  from  \"@ai-sdk/xai\" ; import  { generateImage }  from  \"ai\" ; import  fs  from  \"fs\" ; // Load image and encode as base64 const  imageBuffer  =  fs. readFileSync ( \"photo.png\" ); const  base64Image  =  imageBuffer. toString ( \"base64\" ); const  {  image  }  =  await  generateImage ({     model: xai. image ( \"grok-imagine-image-quality\" ),     prompt: {         text:  \"Render this as a pencil sketch with detailed shading\" ,         images: [ `data:image/png;base64,${ base64Image }` ],     }, }); console. log (image.base64);\n\n...\n\nSAML SSO, role-based access, and audit logging\n\n* * *\n\nLast updated: May 22, 2026\n\nOn this page \n\n* Pricing\n* Image Generation\n* Image Editing\n* Image-to-Video\n* More Capabilities\n* Enterprise Compliance & Security\n\nCopy for LLM Share feedback"

      "url": "https://aitoolsrecap.com/Blog/how-to-organize-projects-inside-grok-2026",

        "TUE, JULY 14, 2026\n\n | [Join Us ★](https://aitoolsrecap.com/JoinUs.aspx?package=free)\n\nIndependent · In‑Depth · Practitioner‑Tested\n\nThe Journal of Artificial Intelligence Tools\n\n Join Us ★\n\n✎ General\n\n# How to Organize Projects Inside Grok (2026)\n\nTurn Grok into your personal AI command center with smart Project Memory, file integration, task tracking, and structured workflows.\n\nBy AIToolsRecap July 11, 2026 6 min read 147 views\n\nHome › Articles › [General](https://aitoolsrecap.com/Articles.aspx?category=general) › How to Organize Projects Inside Grok (2026)\n\nHow to Organize Projects Inside Grok (2026)\n\nGROK PROJECT ORGANIZATION 2026\n\n● **Project Memory:** Persistent context across chats\n\n● **File & Code Management:** Upload, reference, and iterate on documents\n\n● **Structured Workflows:** Tasks, milestones, and knowledge bases\n\n● **Best practice:** One clear project name per initiative\n\n## Setting Up Your First Grok Project\nStart by telling Grok: \"Create new Project: [Project Name]\" and describe the goals, deliverables, and key context. Grok will initialize the memory space and suggest a structure.\n\n## Core Organization Features\n\n**1\\. Project Memory** — Store goals, decisions, research summaries, and preferences.  \n**2\\. File Integration** — Upload documents, spreadsheets, images, or code. Grok can reference them in future sessions.  \n**3\\. Task & Milestone Tracking** — Ask Grok to maintain a running task list or roadmap.  \n**4\\. Knowledge Base** — Build a living document of important facts, style guides, or brand assets.\n\n## Recommended Project Structure\n\n• Overview & Goals  \n• Research & References  \n• Assets & Files  \n• Tasks & Timeline  \n• Decisions Log  \n• Output Deliverables\n\nTo switch projects, simply say \"Switch to Project [Name]\" or \"Load [Project Name]\".\n\nUpdated July 2026 · Grok by xAI\n\nTags\n\nGrok Project Management Productivity AI Workspace xAI 2026\n\nShare this article\n[𝕏 Share on X](https://twitter.com/intent/tweet?text=How%20to%20Organize%20Projects%20Inside%20Grok%20%282026%29%20%E2%80%94%20via%20%40AIToolsRecap&url=https%3A%2F%2Faitoolsrecap.com%2FBlog%2Fhow-to-organize-projects-inside-grok-2026) [LinkedIn](https://www.linkedin.com/sharing/share-offsite/?url=https%3A%2F%2Faitoolsrecap.com%2FBlog%2Fhow-to-organize-projects-inside-grok-2026) [Medium](https://medium.com/@aitoolsrecap) [Instagram](https://www.instagram.com/aitoolsrecap/) [TikTok](https://www.tiktok.com/@aitoolsrecap) [Quora](https://www.quora.com/share?url=https%3A%2F%2Faitoolsrecap.com%2FBlog%2Fhow-to-organize-projects-inside-grok-2026&title=How%20to%20Organize%20Projects%20Inside%20Grok%20%282026%29) ← All Articles\n\n⚑\n\nSpot an inaccuracy?\n\nWe verify facts before publishing and correct errors promptly. How each works, when to use it, transparency gaps documented.\" url: \"https://suprmind.ai/hub/grok/grok-features/\" published: \"2026-05-07T18:21:27+00:00\" modified: \"2026-07-12T14:32:33+00:00\" author: Radomir Basta type: page schema: WebPage language: en-US site_name: Suprmind",
      "publish_date": null,
      "excerpts": [
        "---\ntitle: \"Grok Features 2026: DeepSearch, Think Mode, Companions\"\ndescription: \"Grok features 2026: DeepSearch, Think Mode, Imagine, Companions, voice, memory, projects. How each works, when to use it, transparency gaps documented.\"\nurl: \"https://suprmind.ai/hub/grok/grok-features/\"\npublished: \"2026-05-07T18:21:27+00:00\"\nmodified: \"2026-07-12T14:32:33+00:00\"\nauthor: Radomir Basta\ntype: page\nschema: WebPage\nlanguage: en-US\nsite_name: Suprmind\n---\n\n# Grok Features 2026: DeepSearch, Think Mode, Companions\n\nGrok Features Deep Dive\n\n# How Grok Works: DeepSearch, Think Mode, Companions and More\n\nGrok ships with twelve distinct features split across four categories: research and reasoning, content generation, conversational interfaces, and workspace tools.\n\nThis guide covers what each feature actually does, how it works mechanically, when to use it, when not to, and the documented limitations and transparency gaps.\n\n...\n\nThe mechanism documented in independent sources: when a user starts a new conversation, Grok queries its memory store for relevant prior facts and preferences, then injects those into the conversation context before generating a response. This reduces context-window pressure for long-running users while maintaining personalization.\n\n#### The API gap\n\nThe standard xAI API does not have native cross-session persistent memory as of the research date. Developers building memory features for Grok-based applications must construct their own external memory layer using vector databases (e.g., Pinecone, Weaviate), specialized memory services (Mem0), or in-house infrastructure. ChatGPT and Claude have offered native API memory for over a year; this is a documented gap for Grok in the developer ecosystem.\n\n### User-facing limits\n\n“Asking Grok to forget certain information does not automatically erase it” – manual deletion through settings is required.\nMemory often breaks across conversations (documented in independent reviews of Grok memory behavior). Memory was not available in certain regions at the April 2025 beta launch; specific regions were not disclosed.\n\nFor users who depend on memory continuity, the feature works in the consumer apps. For developers building memory-dependent applications, plan to build the memory layer yourself.\n\n\n\n\n\nProjects and Tasks\n\n## Workspaces are documented. Tasks mechanics are not.\n\nProjects (also called Workspaces) act as containers for related chats, files, and custom instructions. The official description from grok.com/project: “Supercharge Grok with Projects. Create custom workspaces, upload files for smarter chats, and collaborate securely.” Each workspace holds persistent files, conversation history, and custom prompts. Users train the workspace by uploading documents that subsequent chats can reference. Workspaces launched approximately April 12-15, 2025.\n\n#### Projects: tier and use cases\nAccessible across consumer tiers, with Free users getting a basic level. Grok Business at $30/seat/month adds dedicated team workspaces with sharing controls. File upload limits follow the documented 25 MB chat / 48 MB API split.\n\nMost useful for sustained workflows on a defined topic: ongoing client engagements, multi-document research, code review across a repository, or product analysis. Hard limits per project (file count, total storage, depth) are not officially published.\n\n#### Tasks: documented but undocumented\n\nTasks is an automation and scheduling capability accessible at grok.com/tasks. The page renders xAI’s standard marketing copy without a verbatim feature definition. Specific mechanics (trigger types, scheduling syntax, automation depth) are not documented in available official sources.\n\nIndependent sources describe Tasks as available on Free tier and above.\n\n...\n\nMemory is stored outside the context window and selectively injected at conversation start. The standard API does not have native cross-session memory; developers must build their own memory layer.\n\n What are Grok Projects?\n\n +\n\n\n\nWorkspaces that hold persistent files, conversation history, and custom instructions for related chats. Available across consumer tiers; Grok Business adds team sharing at $30/seat/month. Launched April 2025.\n\n What is Grok Build?\n\n +\n\n\n\nA coding agent in pre-launch as of May 2026. Features parallel agent spawning (up to 8 agents) and Arena Mode for tournament-style solution evaluation. Uses Grok 4.3 as underlying model. Tier availability and pricing not yet disclosed. Treat"

        "#### Grok\n\n# Welcome to Grok\n\nGrok is xAI's assistant, available on the web at [grok.com](https://grok.com) and in the iOS and Android apps. Sign in once and your conversations, settings, and subscription stay in sync across every platform.\n\n## What you can do\n\n* **Chat** — ask questions, brainstorm, write, and work through problems in a natural back-and-forth.\n* **Create images and video** with Grok Imagine.\n* **Talk to Grok** hands-free with voice.\n* **Upload files** — PDFs, images, spreadsheets, code, audio, and more — for analysis, extraction, and summarization.\n* **Connect your tools** so Grok can reach your email, files, and calendar right inside a chat. See [Connectors](/grok/connectors).\n\n## Plans and usage\n\nGrok is free to start. Paid SuperGrok plans raise your limits and unlock more across every product, drawing from a single weekly usage allowance you can spend however you like. For how limits, Extra Usage Credits, and billing work, see the [FAQ](/grok/faq).\n## Where to go next\n\n* **[Help & FAQ](/grok/faq)** — billing and subscriptions, accounts, image and video generation, file uploads, and weekly usage limits.\n* **[Connectors](/grok/connectors)** — link Grok to the apps and data you already use.\n* **[Business & Enterprise](/grok/user-guide)** — team workspaces, licenses, and organization controls."

Each plugin extends agent capabilities with tools, resources, and prompts.\n\n> **Important:** Always use the CLI commands below to interact with the marketplace. Do NOT make raw HTTP/API requests — the authentication flow is complex and error-prone. The CLI handles auth, token refresh, and retries automatically.\n\nWhen you need to extend your capabilities with MCP plugins — file system access, database connections, API integrations, or any specialized tools — search the marketplace:\n\n```bash\nnpx -y @lobehub/market-cli mcp search --q \"KEYWORD\"\n```\n\nSee [references/mcp-search.md](../plugins/references/mcp-search.md) for full search options and examples.\n\n---\n\n## View This Plugin\n\nTo get detailed information about this plugin:\n\n```bash\nnpx -y @lobehub/market-cli mcp view howardpen9-grok-mcp\n```\n\nView with comments:\n\n```bash\nnpx -y @lobehub/market-cli mcp view howardpen9-grok-mcp --comments\n```\n\nView a specific version:\n\n```bash\nnpx -y @lobehub/market-cli mcp view howardpen9-grok-mcp --version 0.1.0\n```\n\nSee [references/mcp-view.md](../plugins/references/mcp-view.md) for full view options.\n\n---\n\n## Using This Plugin\n\n### Configuration\n\nMCP plugins communicate via stdio, SSE, or HTTP. Check the plugin's `connectionType` and configure it in your MCP environment accordingly.\n\n**For stdio plugins (most common):**\n\n```json\n{\n  \"mcpServers\": {\n    \"howardpen9-grok-mcp\": {\n      \"command\": \"npx\",\n      \"args\": [\"-y\", \"howardpen9-grok-mcp\"]\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\nRefer to the plugin's documentation for specific configuration instructions.\n\n### After Using\n\n1. Test the plugin's tools and verify they work as expected\n2. Come back and rate / comment on the plugin (see below)\n\n---\n\n## Rate & Comment\n\nAfter using this plugin, leave a rating and comment to help other agents discover high-quality plugins.\n\n### Rate\n\n```bash\nnpx -y @lobehub/market-cli mcp rate howardpen9-grok-mcp --score <1-5>\n```\n\n| Score | Meaning                                                         |\n| ----- | --------------------------------------------------------------- |\n| 5     | Excellent — works perfectly, easy to configure, well documented |\n| 4     | Good — works well with minor issues                             |\n| 3     | Okay — functional but documentation could be clearer            |\n| 2     | Poor — partially works, missing features or confusing setup     |\n| 1     | Broken — doesn't work, errors, or completely unusable           |\n\n### Comment\n\n"

      "url": "https://mcpservers.org/servers/merterbak/Grok-MCP",
      "title": "description: A MCP server for xAI's Grok API, providing access to capabilities including image understanding, image generation, live web search, and reasoning models. title: Grok MCP Server | Awesome MCP Servers image: https://mcpservers.org/api/og?text=Grok%20MCP",
      "publish_date": null,
      "excerpts": [
        "---\ndescription: A MCP server for xAI&#x27;s Grok API, providing access to capabilities including image understanding, image generation, live web search, and reasoning models.\ntitle: Grok MCP Server | Awesome MCP Servers\nimage: https://mcpservers.org/api/og?text=Grok%20MCP\n---\n\n# Grok MCP\n\nA MCP server for xAI's Grok API, providing access to capabilities including image understanding, image generation, live web search, and reasoning models.\n\n[GitHub47](https://github.com/merterbak/Grok-MCP)\n\n## Documentation\n\nRequest update\n\n## Grok-MCP\n\nMCP server for xAI’s Grok API with Web/X search, vision, image/video generation and file support.\n\n[ ![](https://glama.ai/mcp/servers/@merterbak/Grok-MCP/badge) ](https://glama.ai/mcp/servers/@merterbak/Grok-MCP) \n\n## Features\n\n* **Agentic Tool Calling**: Web search, X search, and code execution with multi-step reasoning\n* **Multiple Grok Models**: Access to latest models such as grok-4.5, grok-4.3, grok-build-0.1 and more\n* **Image and Video Generation**: Create images and videos using Grok Imagine\n* **Vision Capabilities**: Analyze images with Grok's vision models\n* **Files API**: Upload, manage, and chat with documents\n* **Stateful Conversations**: Maintain conversation context as id across multiple requests\n* **Local Chat History**: Option to save persistent client side chat history as JSON files in chats/\n\n## Prerequisites\n\n* Python 3.11 or higher\n* xAI API key ([Get one here](https://console.x.ai))\n* [Astral UV](https://docs.astral.sh/uv/getting-started/installation/)\n\n## Installation\n\n1. Clone the repository:\n\n```bash\ngit clone https://github.com/merterbak/Grok-MCP.git\ncd Grok-MCP\n\n```\n\n1. Create a venv environment:\n\n```bash\nuv venv\nsource .venv/bin/activate # macOS/Linux or .venv\\Scripts\\activate on Windows\n\n```\n\n1. Install dependencies:\n\n```bash\nuv sync\n\n```\n\n## Configuration\n\n### Claude Desktop Integration\n\nAdd this to your Claude Desktop configuration file:\n\n```json\n{\n  \"mcpServers\": {\n\"grok\": {\n      \"command\": \"uv\",\n      \"args\": [\n        \"--directory\",\n        \"/path/to/Grok-MCP\",\n        \"run\",\n        \"python\",\n        \"main.py\"\n      ],\n      \"env\": {\n        \"XAI_API_KEY\": \"your_api_key_here\"\n      }\n    }\n  }\n}\n\n```\n\n### Claude Code Integration\n\nRun this command from inside the project directory:\n\n```bash\nclaude mcp add grok-mcp -e XAI_API_KEY=your_api_key_here -- uv run --directory /path/to/Grok-MCP python main.py\n\n```\n\nOr if you have a `.env` file with your key:\n\n```bash\n claude mcp add grok-mcp -- uv run --directory /path/to/Grok-MCP python main.py\n\n```\n\nVerify it's registered:\n\n```bash\nclaude mcp list\n\n```\n\n### Filesystem MCP (Optional)\n\nClaude Desktop can't send uploaded images in the chat to an MCP tool. The easiest way to give access to files directly from your computer is official Filesystem MCP server. After setting it up you’ll be able to just write the image’s file path (such as /Users/mert/Desktop/image."
      ]

        "For more information, see the [Codex MCP documentation](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/main/codex-rs/config.md) .\n\nUse the Copilot CLI to interactively add the Playwright MCP server:\n\n```\n/mcp add\n```\n\nAlternatively, create or edit the configuration file `~/.copilot/mcp-config.json` and add:\n\n```\n{\n  \"mcpServers\": {\n    \"playwright\": {\n      \"type\": \"local\",\n      \"command\": \"npx\",\n      \"tools\": [\n        \"*\"\n      ],\n      \"args\": [\n        \"@playwright/mcp@latest\"\n      ]\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nFor more information, see the [Copilot CLI documentation](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/concepts/agents/about-copilot-cli) .\n\n#### Click the button to install:\n\n#### Or install manually:\n\nGo to `Cursor Settings` \\-> `MCP` \\-> `Add new MCP Server` . Name to your liking, use `command` type with the command `npx @playwright/mcp@latest` . You can also verify config or add command like arguments via clicking `Edit` .\n\nUse the Factory CLI to add the Playwright MCP server:\n\n```\ndroid mcp add playwright \"npx @playwright/mcp@latest\"\n```\n\nAlternatively, type `/mcp` within Factory droid to open an interactive UI for managing MCP servers.\n\nFor more information, see the [Factory MCP documentation](https://docs.factory.ai/cli/configuration/mcp) .\n\nFollow the MCP install [guide](https://github.com/google-gemini/gemini-cli/blob/main/docs/tools/mcp-server.md) , use the standard config above.\n\n#### Click the button to install:\n\nInstall in Goose\n\n#### Or install manually:\n\nGo to `Advanced settings` \\-> `Extensions` \\-> `Add custom extension` . Name to your liking, use type `STDIO` , and set the `command` to `npx @playwright/mcp` . Click \"Add Extension\".\n\nUse the Grok CLI to add the Playwright MCP server:\n\n```\ngrok mcp add playwright -- npx @playwright/mcp@latest\n```\n\nAlternatively, create or edit the configuration file `~/.grok/config.toml` and add:\n\n```\n[mcp_servers.playwright]\ncommand = \"npx\"\nargs = [\"@playwright/mcp@latest\"]\n```\nFor more information, see the [Grok MCP documentation](https://docs.x.ai/build/features/mcp-servers) .\n\nTo add the Playwright MCP server in Junie CLI:\n\n1. Type `/mcp`\n2. Press `Ctrl+A` to add a new MCP server\n3. Select **Playwright** from the list\n\nAlternatively, add to `.junie/mcp/mcp.json` :\n\n```\n{\n  \"mcpServers\": {\n    \"Playwright\": {\n      \"command\": \"npx\",\n      \"args\": [\n        \"-y\",\n        \"@playwright/mcp@latest\"\n      ]\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nFor more information, see the [Junie MCP configuration documentation](https://junie.jetbrains.com/docs/junie-cli-mcp-configuration.html) .\n\nAdd to Kiro\n\nFollow the MCP Servers [documentation](https://kiro.dev/docs/mcp/) . For example in `.kiro/settings/mcp.json` :\n\n```\n{\n  \"mcpServers\": {\n    \"playwright\": {\n      \"command\": \"npx\",\n      \"args\": [\n        \"@playwright/mcp@latest\"\n      ]\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n#### Click the button to install:\n\nAdd MCP Server playwright to LM Studio\n\n#### Or install manually:"
